# Research ledger

## Target

Prove or refute the ordinary theta asymptotic $\vartheta(\overline{H_p})/\sqrt{(p-1)/2}\to1$ through all primes $p\equiv1\pmod4$, where $H_p$ is the subgraph induced on the nonzero squares. [ETH statement](https://randomstrasse101.math.ethz.ch/posts/PaleyGraph/) · [Public attempt record](https://jig.so/p/7) · [Open-problem index](https://clopen.solutions/OP-00041/)

## Routes at the 2026-11-18 checkpoint

| Route | Evidence | Next test or limitation |
|---|---|---|
| Full ordinary theta LP | Two independent support LPs for 27 primes through 10009, with rational enclosures for 20 primes through 1997. The product identity agrees numerically on all of them. [Code](../theta.py) · [Certified cases](../data/theta-certified.csv) · [Larger LPs](../data/theta-large.csv) | Provides finite comparisons and optimal witness data. A finite deviation cannot refute a limit statement. |
| Character weights on allowed edges | The constant, opposite-point and triangle-weighted columns together attain leading constant $1/\sqrt2$, by joint Gauss and twisted Legendre equidistribution. A rational interval replay verifies a strict finite improvement over the optimized constant row at $p=9413$. For $p=200009$, the full degree-eight Chebyshev basis with the opposite-point feature gives $0.71484\sqrt n$ and $0.71434\sqrt n$ for the two sides. [Joint three-column proof](parallel-witness-6.md) · [Larger-basis experiment](../data/witnesses.csv) | The three-column route gives no fixed leading improvement. The numerical degree-eight values provide no asymptotic bound on that larger family. |
| Complementary Fourier smoothing | The local multiplicative convolution formula gives a proof draft for cancellation of the marked coordinate at **every fixed depth**, extending the earlier third-update draft. Inertia at $1$ handles the non-self-dual terms. Projection and interval FFT certify instances up to $p=1000033$. [Fixed-depth proof draft](iteration-parity.md) · [Fourth-update diagnostics](../data/fourth-step.csv) · [Certified intervals](../data/smoothing-certified.csv) | Independently audit the convolution argument. Either a uniform $\ell_2$ bound for the exact interior solution or sublinear growth of the extreme scaled bulk entries of averaged iterates would close the analytic limit at fixed $\tau$. Establish one along a sequence of $\tau$ tending to zero. |
| Square-order control for smoothing | The exact local theta at $q=r^2$ is $r-1$, with normalized limit $\sqrt2$. The fixed-depth inertia argument applies at these orders, so the uniform bound for the exact iterate would fail there. Paired FFT runs show accumulation on characters trivial on the subfield clique. [Proof and comparison](square-order-control.md) · [Data](../data/prime-square-control.csv) | A limit estimate needs prime-order input. The subfield computation identifies one precise mode to exclude. |
| Uniform spectral gap on small frequency sets | A gap on the large-coordinate set of the true iterate would bound its norm. Fejér kernels along powers with sign $+1$ show that an unrestricted gap for sets of size $\epsilon n$ fails along some primes, even on even nonnegative vectors. [Compression criterion and counterexample](square-order-control.md#a-sector-estimate-that-fails-even-at-prime-order) | Target the actual smoothing relation $u_i^{*2}-v_i^{*2}=4w_i$ in any norm estimate. |
| Translation and subgroup concentration | The exact product weights and complementary supports give a log-distance budget for every shift. Hanson–Petridis excludes a majority of the plus spectrum on any subgroup coset of size at most $\sqrt n$ at prime order. Feasible spectra also test high plateaus via reciprocity. [Proof and tests](translation.md) · [Paired data](../data/translation-diagnostics.csv) | Use the identities to study high sets with more general shapes or at smaller peak scales. |
| Power-series control at large smoothing weights | With the fixed-depth inertia closure rules, a rank-squared norm controls every even feedback product, including exceptional Mellin characters. The exact fixed point satisfies $\sqrt n\|v^*\|_\infty\le6$ for $n\ge\tau\ge1024$. [Rank bound and series argument](large-smoothing.md) | The theta certificate needs small $\tau$, where this Taylor-series majorant no longer gives control. |
| Response at the marked coordinate | An exact integral of the two weighted leverage scores gives log-theta, with uniform endpoint bounds. The linearized marked response is localized on the subfield annihilator at small weights in the square-order runs. Fixed-depth inertia gives vanishing first, third and fifth moments of the corresponding local spectral measure. [Integral and resolvent criteria](marked-response.md) · [42-run sweep](../data/marked-response.csv) | Prove balance on compact weight intervals, or control the marked resolvent. The latter needs more than the present linear spectral mass bound at zero and the first few fixed-depth moments. |
| Random weights and projected LP face dimensions | For $k$ sampled labels, the two projected face dimensions sum to the number of distinct labels minus one. The expected difference, weighted by $1/[k(k-1)]$, sums over $k$ to twice the log of normalized theta. Truncation at $\lceil Cn\rceil$ has exponentially small error in $C$ uniformly in the order, up to $1/\lceil Cn\rceil$. The two-label identity agrees with both ordinary theta LPs and numerical integration on eight small orders. [Derivation](random-weight-ranks.md) · [Data](../data/face-ranks.csv) | Compare the signed contributions across sample sizes through primes. The $p=41$ check gives cancellation between the first term and its remainder, so the sign of the first term can be misleading. |
| Exterior signs and localized dimensions for many labels | Strict complementarity gives an exact decomposition of the two face ranks. Cyclotomic reduction and a binomial minor exclude exact localized eigenvectors on any prime-order set of size less than $\sqrt n$. At $k\ge n\log2$ the averaged dimensions contribute $o(1)$ to the expansion, while at a single mark over square fields the minus localized dimension is $n/2-o(n)$. The random-weight remainder also admits an independent compound Poisson model. [Proofs and 3,888 draws](rank-partitions.md) · [Data](../data/rank-partitions.csv) | Prove cancellation of the exterior sign counts and control localized dimensions at sparse supports through primes. The modular proof controls exact eigenvalues, and quantitative near-unit gaps still require separate estimates. |
| Independent weights at sample size proportional to the order | Poisson size bias compares the independent-weight model to $a_k$ with dimension-free $O(k^{-1/2})$ error. Traffic comparison for the damped iteration and Gaussian concentration at fixed positive intensity give $\sup_{k\ge cn}|a_k|\to0$ for every fixed $c>0$ over primes and squares. Independent review checked the transfer to Haar, removal of damping and log concentration. [Proof and comparisons](poisson-smoothing.md) · [Independent audit](parallel-audit.md) | The audit proves at least logarithmic growth of the squared sub-Gaussian constant as the intensity decreases. The iid sparse Poisson test stays positive at total intensity one on square orders. |
| Sparse random supports with fixed masses | Matching the occupied count reduces the collision error of the normalized compound Poisson model to $O((k/n)^2)$. For every $k\to\infty$ with $k=o(n)$, the signed mean reduces to a uniform sample without replacement with a deterministic harmonic mass profile, or to finitely many fixed mass levels to a prescribed accuracy. [Proof and replay](parallel-sparse.md) | Prove sign balance for the random supports. Prescribed square-grid placements of the same profile have a nonzero limit even though the compression of $K$ to the marks tends to zero. |
| Sparse random supports at depth two and at the optimum | Leaving one mark out and comparing two orthogonal rows with a Gaussian give a second-update signed mean of $O_\tau(\sqrt{k/n}+\sqrt{\sum_j m_j^2})$. One linear cost per sign also bounds the actual signed optimum at zero floor. The rational replay gives $-0.133<B_2<-0.113$ at $p=101$ for the harmonic masses. [Proof and replay](parallel-random-2.md) | Estimate either the stationary residual pairing or the two optimal linear costs for growing sparse samples. Controlling this tail of the rank expansion still leaves the contribution of the smaller sample sizes. |
| Dual majorants and adaptive reweighting of sparse marks | The square-root cost is a signed log optimum over reweightings penalized by relative entropy. An exact square-field rectangle has a vanishing signed log value and a diverging one-cost enclosure at $k\asymp n^{3/4}$. At uniform injections the projection-plus-constant certificate has a direct $O(\sqrt{\log n})$ upper bound, and no rescaling of that certificate keeps it bounded with high probability. [Proof, controls and LP replay](parallel-cost-3.md) | Bound adaptive reweighting for uniform prime injections, or use the signed optimum directly. The rectangle uses prescribed locations. |
| Sparse marks and two-frequency geometry | The signed log objective has variance $O(\log^2(k+1)/k)$ at any growing sparse scale. A common-barycenter coupling removes repeated rows and columns at cost $O(k/r)$ at square order. For two marks, the signed rank term integrates the log support ratio against a positive slope measure. Off-diagonal LPs reduce the sample interval at $p=1997$ from width $0.169$ to $0.0030$. [Sparse geometry](sparse-mark-geometry.md) · [Support identity and comparisons](two-mark-support.md) · [118 rows](../data/two-mark-frontier.csv) | Bound the mean signed optimum on sparse prime samples. The off-diagonal comparisons have sampling uncertainty above the remaining mesh error at the larger prime orders. |
| Fixed damping with finitely many marks | The tame-convolution argument extends to translated source fields and arbitrary masses on a bounded number of marks. It gives vanishing signed log tests at the exact fixed point for each fixed damping factor, uniformly over the marks, at primes and squares. An exact block inverse separates the two sources of the marked value. [Proof draft and square comparison](sparse-damping.md) | Control the undamped stationary norm or the mixed pairing through primes. The square-order one-mark theta forces divergence of the stationary norm even though every fixed-damping limit vanishes. |
| Möbius conjugation and subgroup concentration | On even Fourier vectors $K=C(R-J)C$ with $R$ a half-period shift. In square fields $C$ sends the positional subfield indicator to a union of two subgroups. At prime order an incidence estimate gives overlap $O(p^{-1/20})$ between normalized square-root-scale subgroup indicators in the two bases. [Proof and arithmetic input](mobius.md) · [Exact overlap counts](../data/mobius-overlaps.csv) | The comparison controls flat subgroup shapes and intersections of sets with bounded multiplicative doubling. A general slow-mode estimate still needs control of vectors outside these structured families. |
| Slow modes near subgroup cosets | A compression to a fixed number of frequency subgroup cosets has only finitely many high-eigenvalue blocks at the critical scale. Approximate null vectors supported there have arbitrary phases, but must spread across critical cosets after Möbius transformation over prime fields. Arbitrary supports at the smallest possible scale must be flat in magnitude and nearly saturate the Jacobi entry bound. [Analysis](subgroup-modes.md) · [Compression and response data](../data/subgroup-modes.csv) | Relate the actual stationary modes to these constraints, or bound the Jacobi deficit on arbitrary small supports. |
| Endpoint concentration on critical frequency sets | Xi's Jacobi discrepancy estimate forces $E(S)/|S|^2\to\infty$ and repeated differences for almost all pairs if a smallest-scale slow mode exists. The stationary diagonal approaches a single sign on the support. Any translation overlap exceeding one half by a fixed amount must be asymptotically full. If $E(S)/|S|^3$ exceeds one half by a fixed amount, the support is close to a bounded union of exact cosets. Heuristic searches reproduce the square-order subgroup controls and find a deficit of $0.420173$ at $p=1000033$. [Proof and comparison](critical-sets.md) · [Runs](../data/critical-sets.csv) | Control sets with many intermediate multiplicities or derive stronger energy concentration in the stationary model. |
| Long-order progressions and single subgroup cosets | On a progression of length at most $B n/\sqrt q$, steps of order at least $64B n/\sqrt q$ give a compression gap. With order much larger than $n/\sqrt q$, the gap extends to a bounded number of separated intervals with the same step in one coset. For single subgroup cosets at the critical scale, the exact sign second moment leaves at most two self-inverse positional cosets. [Progression gap](progression-gap.md) · [Subgroup second moment](subgroup-modes.md#which-scalar-blocks-can-approach-norm-one) | Control supports with more general shapes and address the remaining self-inverse cosets at prime order. |
| Higher-rank proper progressions | A bounded-rank proper frequency box of volume at most $B n/\sqrt q$ has a compression gap if all side lengths grow and its generated subgroup is larger by a sufficiently large fixed factor. Poisson summation yields a continuous or finely sampled direction on which a near-extremal vector would force a sign bias exceeding the Jacobi Fourier bound. The rank-two comparisons include energy near $4|S|^3/9$. [Proof](progression-boxes.md) · [28 comparisons](../data/progression-boxes.csv) | Obtain such a cover for actual high sets, or extend the arithmetic to boxes that fill a substantial fraction of the generated subgroup. |
| Bounded-doubling critical supports | Decomposing a proper coset progression over its subgroup and using a mixed continuous and discrete sampling estimate shows that near-unit compressions with bounded doubling require a cover by a bounded number of critical subgroup cosets. Ford's shifted-prime divisor bound excludes those subgroups on a relative-density-one set of primes. On the same prime set every sufficiently slow direction in the full stationary linearization must leave positive squared mass outside such a cover. [Compression proof and transfer](coset-progression-gap.md) | Control the exterior mass that couples to the mark, or handle growing doubling and the exceptional primes. |
| Marked response under localized activation changes | An exact matrix differential equation bounds the change at the mark from arbitrary changes on a set whose reference compression has a gap. A clipped fixed-depth background supplies a delocalized marked column. In the prescribed-diagonal test, the square subfield response tends to $2/3$ and the long-order progression response tends to zero. [Proof](response-locality.md) · [Runs](../data/response-shift.csv) | Relate the actual stationary diagonal to a bounded background away from exceptional sets and locate those sets. |
| Residual decay and true stationary tails | The averaged residual is $O(\sqrt W\,t^{-1/4})$ uniformly in field size. At stationarity, a strongly coupled marked band at width $O(q^{-1/2})$ forces positive normalized $\ell_1$ mass on the high set. When $\tau\log q\to0$, the entire critical band contributes $o(1)$ if $\|v^*\|_2^2=o(\sqrt q)$. At critical support size the compression gap is $O(q^{-1/2})$ and the energy is at least of order $|S|^{5/2}$. [Residual bound](iteration-parity.md#residual-decay-without-an-estimate-on-the-fixed-point) · [Stationary estimates](stationary-tails.md) · [Prime and square runs](../data/stationary-modes.csv) | Control the stationary pairing or rule out a large stationary tail at prime order. The inverse contribution from larger spectral widths also needs control. |
| Stationary resolvent sum rule | An exact anticommutator identity bounds the signed cutoff error by $O(\sqrt{\tau+(T_\eta)_+})$, with $T_\eta$ the mixed low-pass correlation of the stationary total spectrum with the mark. The probe measures cancellation of the positive and negative retained parts and a strong mixed correlation at the square-order control. [Identity and criterion](resolvent-sum-rule.md) · [60 windows](../data/response-windows.csv) | Find widths at which both the regularized signed response and the positive mixed correlation vanish through all primes. |
| Signed stationary cutoff | The source identity $Hv=ae+b$ bounds the signed cutoff difference by the filtered signed stationary spectrum $\langle v,R_\eta e\rangle/a$ plus $O(\sqrt\tau)$. Its general norm bound is $O(\tau+\eta\|v\|_2^2)$. The signed correlation stays small in a tested prime window where the total-spectrum correlation is substantial. [Signed transfer](resolvent-sum-rule.md#using-the-signed-stationary-spectrum) · [Window comparison](../data/response-windows.csv) | Prove cancellation of the signed correlation and the regularized marked response at a common choice of widths. |
| Diagonal damping of the marked inverse | For $H_t=K-tPDP$, every fixed $t<1$ gives marked cancellation on fixed-depth Picard diagonals via a Neumann series and the even-product inertia rule. An iterated-limit normalized $\ell_2$ approximation of the actual diagonal would suffice for this part without a uniform sup bound on the reference columns. The exact removal identity uses the scalar $(1-t)\langle u,H_t^{-1}e\rangle$, which is small in the measured prime comparison even when the companion pairing with $v$ is substantial. [Proof and comparison](diagonal-damping.md) · [60 runs](../data/response-damping.csv) | Prove normalized diagonal approximation and uniform control of the scalar pairing as damping is removed. |
| Capped stationary Fourier mass | Fixed-depth inertia cancels the capped feedback uniformly over all rows and balances the two capped means. If these tests transfer to stationarity, a persistent peak requires positive tail mass on both sides and a difference set of positive density. At cap $8$, the prime comparisons have zero excess and the square comparisons have a substantial one-sided excess and cap imbalance. [Proof and comparison](bulk-caps.md) · [192 rows](../data/bulk-caps.csv) | Transfer the bounded cap tests to the prime stationary solution and bound the excess of either side or the density of the difference set. |
| Bulk logarithmic mean along the weight path | Mixed complementary products give $\gamma_0'(\tau)=-\tau g_p'(\tau)$ for the mean $g_p$ of the bulk hyperbolic coordinates. Integration reduces the theta limit to cancellation of the signed bulk mean on a compact weight range. The exact mass bound controls the tails of the log test uniformly, so transferring only its bounded odd truncations is sufficient. The square controls show a persistent negative mean in the low-weight regime. [Proof and comparison](bulk-logarithm.md) · [198 rows](../data/log-means.csv) | Prove cancellation of the stationary bulk log mean or its integral through all primes. |
| One-sided drift of averaged iterates | Exact telescoping against the positive stationary spectra and Jensen's inequality give separate upper and lower theta tests. A persistent deviation forces energy of the corresponding stationary spectrum on high signed coordinates of explicit iterates, of density $O(1/T)$ at depth $T$. The larger square has positive-side correlation $0.334$ at depth $512$ and both correlations at the nearby prime are about $0.0052$. [Proof and LP comparison](signed-drift.md) · [96 rows](../data/signed-drift.csv) | Control the signed pairing at prime order or find small opposite-support LP majorants for the explicit iteration cost. |
| Explicit signed LP majorants | Translates of the constant witness give a majorant with a threshold and a mean determined by the largest $O(\sqrt p)$ projected entries. The resulting feasible spectra give 96 rational upper bounds without solving the stationary equation. [Construction](iterate-majorants.md) · [128 comparisons](../data/iterate-majorants.csv) | Bound the prime-order top sums after the field-first limit, or use a majorant that exploits more of the shape of the high set. |
| Coset majorants for signed iterate costs | Averaging within a frequency coset gives an exact mass bound from the opposite theta of the corresponding positional subgroup. Combining that bound with point caps gives a sorting repair, and the full quotient mask gives a sparse LP. A prescribed prime-order plateau costs far less under the coset bounds, whereas the square plus plateau retains its point-repair cost. [Proof and comparison](coset-majorants.md) · [70 runs](../data/coset-majorants.csv) | Find a structural cover for actual costly frequencies or control more dispersed costs. |
| Uniform scaled fields at fixed depth | A compact-group polynomial rank bound and an algebra that includes the bounded-sum Mellin errors give a draft uniform sup bound at every individual depth and positive weight. They also give equality up to $o(1)$ of the two explicit repair costs as the field order grows at fixed depth. [Proof draft](uniform-fixed-depth.md) · [Fixed-depth finite comparisons](../data/majorant-depth-control.csv) | Audit the arithmetic inputs and control the large-depth profile through primes. The square-order obstruction remains in this norm. |
| Translated cosets and the mark | On a union of a bounded number of critical frequency cosets disjoint from the annihilator itself, the marked response change at zero background is $O(q^{-1/2})$ for arbitrary diagonal magnitude, even with near-unit compression. An extension handles clipped fixed-depth backgrounds with an identity-block gap. [Coupling proof](coset-response.md) · [Coset comparison](../data/response-cosets.csv) | Transfer the background estimates to the true stationary diagonal. |
| Unshifted coset and sign defects | For $b\sim\sqrt q$ the exact formula gives supremum $2/(k+2)+o(1)$ at zero background along a fixed-deficit subsequence, where $k=b-\max_{C\in Q/B}|F(C)|$. The response vanishes precisely if $k\to\infty$. Growing defect is also sufficient on the clipped fixed-depth background. The arithmetic proof now supplies this growth at prime order when $b\ge\sqrt p-O(1)$. The three square controls have deficit 1. [Exact formula and replay](unshifted-cosets.md) | Transfer the comparison to the true stationary diagonal and handle sizes approaching the critical scale from below with an unbounded difference. |
| Bounded-defect arithmetic | ChatGPT derived every coset autocorrelation and exhibited an integer model with fixed deficit. Using Sanders's theorem, the local proof decomposes the residual square coset sums into a bounded sum of coset indicators. The vanishing half of the Jacobi transform modulo $p$ yields a rational function with a pole and too many repeated values. This proves a uniform gap tending to infinity below $\sqrt p$ for the sign sums. The exact scan covers 12,516 cosets from 7,472 prime–subgroup pairs. [Arithmetic proof and comparison](coset-defects.md) · [Scan](../data/coset-defects.csv) | Obtain stronger bounds at sizes below $\sqrt p$ and relate the arithmetic result to general stationary supports. |
| Signed stationary bands and critical coset unions | A Schur comparison controls eigenvalues of each sign from the corresponding one-sided high set alone. The arithmetic gap on the diagonal of each coset block excludes true width-$O(p^{-1/2})$ bands if that set lies in a bounded union of cosets of total size $m_0+o(\Delta_p)$ up to $o(\Delta_p)$ extra points. Quantitative translation rounding also excludes critical high sets with energy above $(1/2+\delta)|S|^3$. [Proof and comparison](signed-coset-bands.md) · [Band data](../data/coset-bands.csv) | Control critical supports of intermediate energy or prove that strongly marked stationary bands admit covers at the required precision. |
| A prime-field stationary mass gap outside three cosets | Phase alignment on at most three frequency cosets would yield a Paley clique exceeding the Hanson–Petridis bound if those cosets carried almost all the normalized spectral mass. At small smoothing weight the stationary product identity then forces $\Omega(\sqrt n)$ exterior high coordinates. At high-set size $(1+o(1))m_0$, the resulting additive energy bound is $(5/8+o(1))|S|^3$, without needing the finer size correction $o(\Delta_p)$. [Proof and exact square control](parallel-stationary.md) | Determine whether a strongly coupled slow mode puts substantial mass on the required exterior high coordinates. The mass bound alone gives no spectral cancellation for their contribution. |
| Finite phases at the critical coset size | A fixed number of cosets with compression norm tending to one and total size at most $(1+o(1))m_0$ must have bounded displacement order in the quotient. A feasible spectrum concentrated there gives exact signs and a Galois-saturated Fourier bound on the finite quotient. Exact checks give a mass gap through eight cosets for general feasible spectra and eleven for even spectra. The stationary energy bound at critical size is $(13/24+o(1))|S|^3$. [Proof and exact replays](critical-coset-phases.md) | Control modes with mass beyond the finite-phase covers, or extend the cyclic difference bound to arbitrary fixed ranks. |
| Interaction of a coset cover and its exterior in the marked inverse | A resolvent identity expresses the interaction as two cross terms. At the actual stationary diagonal it is bounded by the weighted inverse-column changes on the opposite regions. An exact square model has an order-one interaction even with uniformly bounded exterior activation. The measured interaction at $389^2$ and $\tau=0.01$ is $0.55734$, versus $0.00071$ at the paired prime. [Identity, model and comparisons](coset-activation.md) | Bound the cross terms and the individual restricted responses uniformly at decreasing floors through primes. |
| Focusing on a pair by an exterior activation | Even a mean-zero even exterior of norm at most $1/16$ can concentrate the marked inverse column on two frequencies, through all large primes and squares. For an actual stationary split with $|S|=o(n^{1/4})$, the contribution of the inside-only column to the exterior cross term is $o(1)$. [Proof and comparisons](exterior-column.md) · [Data](../data/exterior-focus.csv) | Use stationarity to control the exterior-only column on the chosen support. Small compression and centering by themselves allow strong Jacobi-product correlations. |
| Sitewise attenuation of the actual stationary diagonal | Two exact source pairings show that deleting $S$ changes the stripped marked entry by $O(\tau+\lVert v_S\rVert_2^2)$ and leaves $O(\sqrt\tau+\lVert v_S\rVert_2)$ column norm on the deleted coordinates. A completed square gives the weaker sufficient condition $\langle v_S,(x_E)_S\rangle_+=o(1)$. [Proof and comparisons](stationary-attenuation.md) | Construct an attenuated background with a small marked response and verify one of the two transfer criteria. A cover can have a small relative share of the stationary squared norm and still carry order-one unnormalized energy. |
| Half-angle inverse and midpoint residual | At $B_i=\tanh(\gamma_i/2)$ with a stripped mark the inverse column is exactly $v/(u_0-2\sqrt{\tau/n})$. This raises the unconditional eigenvalue floor to order $\sqrt{\tau/n}$ and puts the near-endpoint activation set at size $O_\tau(\sqrt n)$ at width $n^{-1/2}$. A midpoint resolvent also bounds trial marker error by one weighted residual, with a full Fourier LP certificate for its unknown weights. At square order with $\tau=1/n$, $B_{1/2}$ keeps marked limit $1/3$ and any exponent $\alpha<1/6$ gives $-1$. [Proof and replay](stationary-secant.md) | Bound the weighted trial residual or the signed half-angle response at primes. The spectral size bound is within a constant factor of the critical size. |
| One-sided deletion and removal of damping | For same-sign changes the full stationary leverage column bounds the signed response change and the exterior column on the deleted set. A gap in just one Fourier sector controls the corresponding side of the damping error. At square order the true stripped deletion of the positive bulk changes the limiting response by $4/3$, and the ordered damping error is $9/8$. [Proof and comparisons](parallel-coupling-2.md) | Bound the actual leverage overlap or one-sided sector norm at primes. The damping limit and the response after deletion require separate estimates. |
| Ordered changes and the exterior inverse endpoint | An exact pairing with the opposite stationary spectrum gives the exterior weighted bulk norm after division by $\sqrt{1+\sigma m_E}$ up to $O(\sqrt\tau)$. A same-sign deletion with a persistent response cost and $1+\sigma m_E\to0$ forces the compressed exterior inverse to approach the relevant endpoint, even as its marked column on the deletion set vanishes. [Proof and replay](stationary-ordered.md) | A uniform prime-field gap for the actual exterior would remove this mechanism. Signed response changes at interior exterior marks still need control. |
| Exterior compression and a one-sided marked envelope | A signed cap on the retained activation transfers a gap from $K_{SS}$ to the exterior inverse compression, allowing the opposite sign to saturate. An exact weighted extension form handles retained same-sign highs and gives a true square example with $\lVert K_{SS}\rVert=o(1)$ but collapsing exterior gap. For a general reference, an exceptional-set inverse form and a one-sided hyperbolic error off the set bound the full marked response. | Fixed-depth reference and normalized defect modes | An exact weighted extension covers nonzero reference entries and singular compressions. The reference form at bounded activation is equivalent to a form on the zero-background compression with feedback-modified data. A translated square coset can have vanishing zero-reference form and nonvanishing bounded-reference form through the constant-direction feedback. Puncturing the coset removes the marked obstruction while the gap still tends to zero. [Proof and checks](parallel-reference-4.md) | Control constant and off-identity positional overlaps of the normalized low-gap modes on the true prime exceptional sets. |
| Common-time signed response | The stripped inverse is the time integral of a signed difference of two squared norms on the same trajectory. A tail identity and a quadratic-form certificate permit cancellation between large unsigned terms. The square-order signed tail after fixed time $T$ tends to $4/3-e^{-T}$ on the full and half-angle diagonals. [Proof and comparisons](parallel-sign-4.md) · [Half-angle tail](stationary-secant.md#square-order-failure-under-stronger-hyperbolic-attenuation) | Prove finite-time balance and decay of the actual signed tail at primes. A full-space singular-channel estimate does not match the small observed prime tails. |
| Marked-source contraction defect and low dissipation modes | The marked Krylov orbit has a contractive coefficient synthesis map. A tent average gives dissipation norm $O(1/T)$, and the half-angle stationary column has bounded dissipation energy. The signed high-mode error is $O((T\eta)^{-1})$ and the remaining rank is $O(n\eta^2/\tau)$. One observed positive mode accounts for over $99.9\%$ of the matched square tail at $193^2$. [Proof and 36 comparisons](stationary-defect-tail.md) | Bound marked response on the two low signed spaces in the field-first limit. Prove compact-time parity for the true half-angle diagonal. |
| Localized symmetric window and moving square cutoffs | The exact half-angle source reduces the hard inverse window to one signed pairing on a measured saturation set, with error $O(\tau^{1/4}\eta/\delta)$. A time average has a spatial error $O(\tau^{1/4}/(T\delta))$. At square order with $\tau=1/n$, a signed contribution of $4/3$ survives at window $n^{-1/7}$ or time $n^{1/7}$. [Proof and window checks](parallel-half-modes-5.md) | Estimate the retained signed pairing through primes. A bound for large growing times does not establish the field-first interchange. |
| Reciprocal spectra on the two signed blocks | The coupling of the two stationary inverse directions is $O(\sqrt\tau+p^{-1/2})$ before normalization, while their dissipation energies are of order one. If a true same-sign high set has near-minimum cardinality and floors tend to zero, its signed half-angle mark tends to $1/3$ and the other high set is at least twice as large. A phase-margin rectangle test uses the prime-field Hanson–Petridis theorem. [Proof and controls](parallel-half-arithmetic-5.md) | Rule out such phase configurations at primes or bound the coupling across the full slow spaces. The analytic bounds here are ordinary mathematical arguments with cited inputs; the specific independently reviewed and replayed results are identified above. The code certifies individual-prime intervals by positive semidefinite Fourier witnesses. For $p=1000033$ the normalized interval is $[1.000325,1.001326]$. Both types of evidence leave the target open.

The first parallel pass leaves two different estimates to pursue. The stationary route has an arithmetic constraint absent at square order. To reach the marked limit it needs quantitative coupling of the required exterior high mass to the strongly marked spectral band. The sparse route can now vary the support while keeping a bounded deterministic mass palette. Its next comparison is the expected signed log optimum for a uniform injection, with the prescribed square-grid placements as a control for estimates based only on the mark compression.

The second parallel pass separates three questions. The [three-point cap](parallel-arithmetic-2.md) excludes near-critical supports whose nonzero overlaps are all at most order $q^{1/4}$. It leaves intermediate overlaps, even after the finite coset exclusions. The [one-sided stationary bounds](parallel-coupling-2.md) and the [attenuation identities](stationary-attenuation.md) identify explicit pairings to estimate at the true stationary diagonal. The square-order limits quantify the failure of removing the full positive high set or interchanging the inverse and damping limits. The [random restriction calculation](parallel-random-2.md) applies at prime and square orders through the second update. Its linear support bounds give a separate way to target the actual optimum.

The third pass raises the [arithmetic overlap threshold](parallel-overlap-3.md) and separates capped and uncapped exteriors in the [weighted compression problem](parallel-exterior-3.md). The [ordered stationary comparisons](stationary-ordered.md#numerical-comparison) show that removing either sign can change the marked entry substantially even in the sampled primes, where the two changes nearly cancel. Equation (4) in that note proves that both full-sign changes cannot vanish in the bounded-energy regime. The [square-root dual](parallel-cost-3.md) tests a larger set of reweighted profiles than the original sampled signed objective. Its exact square comparison separates those two objectives.

The fourth pass gives the [half-angle stationary column](stationary-secant.md) and the [common signed time evolution](parallel-sign-4.md). They permit a signed tail test on a linearization with a larger eigenvalue floor. The [fixed-depth reference form](parallel-reference-4.md) isolates a separate constant-direction contribution in small-gap compressions. The [paired-anchor count](parallel-five-4.md) clears the previous fifth-anchor threshold and improves the necessary overlap exponent to $3/7$.

The fifth pass separates the [low signed dissipative modes](stationary-defect-tail.md) and the [hard inverse window](parallel-half-modes-5.md) without a uniform stationary norm bound. The [reciprocal-spectrum calculation](parallel-half-arithmetic-5.md) distinguishes near-minimum high sets from arbitrary critical-scale high sets. The [Krylov replay](parallel-half-krylov-5.md) checks genuine cancellation at finite primes by comparing a signed interval with lower bounds on both unsigned tails.

The sixth pass relates the two positive definite [marked and constant block responses](stationary-mark-constant.md) to the actual stationary marker. The square limits and the marked comparison at $1008001$ give a second way to distinguish extreme and apparently balanced regimes. The [capped commutator](parallel-finite-time-6.md) makes the compact-time transfer a separate signed estimate. The [reflected energy argument](parallel-extreme-6.md) excludes structured near-minimum supports at logarithmic resolution. The [joint Legendre law](parallel-witness-6.md) closes the constant, opposite-point and triangle witness family.

The seventh pass compares two separate tests of critical concentration. The [affine rank test](parallel-low-energy-7.md) needs no spectral assumption at zero floor, and the [additive compression](additive-neighbor-compression.md) would reach approximate support at ordinary floors given a uniform gap. Its two signs also have exact cyclic matrix representations on split and nonsplit tori. The [reciprocal extension](parallel-extension-7.md) and [cross-floor certificate](parallel-temperature-7.md) target the theta limit itself through, respectively, a one-sided weighted overlap and a signed sum across a floor mesh. The main conversation replayed 8,782 small exact rank checks, the five square saturation counts, and both stationary identities at $101$ and $529$.

The eighth pass removes the floor parameter from the [negative-part route](negative-pairing.md) and checks its prime Fejér obstruction against the actual finite-time recurrence. It compares this route with [variance saturation on the true floor path](parallel-floor-8.md) and [joint-offset cancellation for the torus kernels](parallel-torus-8.md). The main conversation independently checked five prime Fejér patterns, both floor budgets at $101$ and $529$, the cyclic square transfer on both parity types, and the repaired interval rows.

The ninth pass audited the two model returns. [Astra's switching loss](astra-iterate-8.md) extends the checked orbit estimate from a terminal norm to a dyadic mass bound. [Fable's audit](fable-audit-9.md) retains the qualitative strict prime bound and rejects its proposed equality classification at square order. The main conversation introduced the [reciprocal quadratic path](quadratic-theta.md) and independently replayed the residual bracket, the two-width switching identity and both interval counterexamples. The two Codex studies then identified [support re-entry](parallel-quadratic-support-9.md) and a [signed finite-cutoff criterion](parallel-quadratic-limit-9.md).

The tenth pass computed the [marked kernel atom and positivity cost](endpoint-norm.md) on the actual endpoint support. The exact extension at $p=97$ requires a positive correction. The endpoint tangent test is equivalent to a dual extension with coordinatewise upper bounds. The [prime flatness criterion](parallel-endpoint-10.md) and its pure Jacobi input led to [square endpoint uniqueness](square-endpoint-uniqueness.md). An independent audit checked the proof, exhausted the small cases and replayed all 16 modular rank certificates. [Astra's moving-width estimate](astra-quadratic-10.md) excludes near-idempotent probability spectra at critical concentration for primes, and its order-49 interval counterexample was independently replayed. The uniform marked kernel bound and the signed transfer for dispersed spectra remain open.

The eleventh pass replaces the first-switch threshold by the [full one-sided mark integral](endpoint-tangent.md). The first-piece proof, the exact square-minus curve, and the $p=809$ ball certificate passed independent review. Forty-two ball enclosures sample the path without inferring supports. The [leakage calculation](parallel-kernel-11.md) gives a second test on the endpoint itself and a lower bound for bulk support density when $n$ has a prime factor of bounded index. The centered term still requires an estimate. [Astra's cross-convolution reduction](astra-convolution-11.md) isolates signed covariance and certifies two order-49 controls with different scalar moment behavior. Both controls were independently replayed.

The twelfth pass reduces the fourth-moment route to an [allowed-energy test at one width](quadratic-fourth.md). The proof, an exact low-order certificate and the prime Fejér obstruction were checked independently. Seventy-two ball enclosures compare the actual energies and moments, including the large square at $389^2$. The [folded rank argument](parallel-sampling-12.md) improves the fixed-cofactor density term and gives injective sampling controls that separate rank from marked stability. The exact $p=29$ certificate was independently replayed. [Astra's centered projection](astra-fourth-12.md) isolates the sign carried by high coordinates in the square control. Both strict orthant certificates and the 49 numerical comparisons were independently replayed. The uniform prime energy and centered sampling estimates remain open.

The thirteenth pass completes and checks the [fixed positive width proof](parallel-fixed-width-13.md) against the local convolution and Mellin theorems. The separate [log-star cutoff proof](slow-width.md) quantifies the complexity cost and passes independent review. The [mean and marked compression formula](quadratic-compression.md) was checked on 64 rows, with all 40 ball intervals independently replayed. [Astra's current-width source criterion](astra-energy-13.md) treats the larger-theta energy alone and gives a necessary sampling failure with controlled global norm. Its two strict orthants and rational certificates were independently replayed. The moving-width prime energy bound remains open.

The fourteenth pass proves that [weighted losses at the mark](parallel-complexity-14.md) give balance whenever $r\log_2^*q\to\infty$. The Mellin estimate and replay passed independent review. The [one-sided gradient split](quadratic-gap.md) certifies the energy using a single larger-width trial, and improves the direct capped bound in all 34 interval rows through order $1000033$. [Astra's actual-support cuts](astra-sampling-14.md) use complementarity on every active coordinate. All 50 numerical rows and the two algebraic-width orthants at $p=13$ were replayed. The Fejér example passes the maximum-norm residual test but forces every split of that trial to pay a total cost above the needed $r\log_2^*p$ scale. The remaining sufficient bound is $o(\log_2^*p)$ on the larger-theta energy at $p^{-1/3}$.

The fifteenth pass compares the [directional certificate](quadratic-angle.md), the [attenuated dual](parallel-transport-15.md), and [Astra's actual-support cut](astra-cuts-15.md). Independent review checked the 92 interval comparisons, the 64 floating-point transport rows, and the 50 cut rows with a separate two-sided algebraic-width replay at $p=13$. On the larger-theta side, a signed surplus bound of $o(p^{-2/3}\log_2^*p)$ on one actual high-level cut would imply the sufficient energy bound. An alternative would control the ordered defect of the projected slow-width reference using its available mean reserve. Both prime estimates remain open.

The sixteenth pass tests [projected positive repair](parallel-defect-16.md) and [simultaneous Fourier certificates](endpoint-mixture.md). The 32 repair enclosures were independently replayed. Sixteen prime rows fit the available mean reserve, while eight square plus rows attain the subfield optimum and exceed the reserve. The multi-reference experiment records 259 margins and gains from testing full forbidden residual directions. [Astra's source duality](astra-source-16.md) gives exact additive rank at the cubic width and a signed-sector obstruction to quantitative conditioning. The [square-indexed orbit](prime-additive-rigidity.md) extends the rank statement to the square-root width and other rational-power widths. Both prime-order estimates remain open.

The seventeenth pass verifies [four clipped projection steps](parallel-repair-17.md) with the true reference errors enclosed, including the million-order prime. [Astra's localized response](astra-preimage-17.md) preserves the root and quantifies the low-layer contribution. Its 32 numerical rows and two exact-width sign certificates were replayed. The [cyclotomic determinant bound](rational-endpoint.md) covers real testers with finitely many fractional classes when the frequency-group order has a large prime factor. The current polynomial bounds and finite repair margins leave the all-prime limit open.

The eighteenth pass compares [minimum mean, direct ratio optimization and a scalar convolution resolvent](parallel-ratio-18.md). It records 134 bounds from rounded corrections and 36 rational lower bounds from the resolvent. Direct ratio optimization improves the minimum-mean lower bound by more than $10^{-9}$ in all 40 tested prime settings. The scalar solve adds at most $5.38\cdot10^{-7}$ over the best translated method at the tested thresholds. The [tail split](parallel-tail-18.md) independently isolates the starting load and background injection, with 864 main and 288 smaller-width floating-point rows replayed. [Astra's low-layer counterexample](astra-low-layer-18.md) gives two strict prime KKT certificates and an abstract positive two-sector family. All 32 numerical rows and five certificate rows replayed. The prime-aspect estimate remains open on both routes.

The nineteenth pass eliminates background injection from the [thresholded repair](threshold-repair-19.md) and proves a finite positive comparison with a support-size barrier. The main sweep has 68 interval bounds, the narrow-width million-order comparison adds four, and two support certificates rule out the positive inverse at the cutoffs used by the signed candidates at $p=153649$. At $p=1000033$ and $s=1/64$, signed iteration improves the normalized lower on the conjecture side from $0.98521773$ to $0.99019865$ and brings both sides within the mean reserve. [The transfer construction](parallel-cap-19.md) excludes a uniform one-step gap with only a vanishing-maximum-atom condition and specifies a flat rectangle problem for successive tight steps. [Astra's Picard comparisons](astra-background-19.md) were replayed on 126 diagnostic rows and two interval certificates. Uniform signed repair or localized prime control remains to be proved.

The twentieth pass pins the mark, identifies the minimum signed defect with an opposite Fourier witness, and proves a [uniform signed contraction](anchored-repair-20.md#a-uniform-single-step-gap-for-concentrated-prime-defects) when the effective support is near critical and the high layer's additive energy exceeds half its maximal scale by a fixed amount. The arithmetic input is the [nonalternating coset gap](parallel-rectangle-20.md), checked against the hypergeometric and rich-intersection bounds and with independent finite Fourier normalizations. The anchored replays contain 75 interval theta bounds and two exact square-defect certifications. At $p=1000033$ the pinned near-one scan certifies $0.99742075$ on the conjecture side. [Astra's eight signed trajectories](astra-signed-20.md) replayed independently. The broad-support and low-energy prime regimes remain unresolved.

The twenty-first pass obtains an [exact large-penalty characterization of the broad margin](soft-tail-21.md) and verifies 48 primal and 15 dual interval bounds, including both signs at the million-order prime and exact square decay. The finite positive dual bounds at mean $0.96$ check the rounded cost as well as the anchor. [Astra](astra-broad-21.md) retains the actual mean reserve in a one-sided reference-tail test. The [cross-ratio analysis](parallel-crossratio-21.md) lowers the needed moment to the strong $3/2$ endpoint for anchor averages, with power savings for individual overlaps and an exact exceptional square family. Both supplemental checks were independently replayed. Neither the prime large-penalty decay nor the endpoint moment for the actual prime profiles has been proved.

The twenty-second pass derives [one-sided quadratic reciprocity](one-sided-quadratic-22.md), an explicit fixed-mark defect certificate from any positive trial, and sufficient intermediate-width estimates for the full conjecture. The 56 Arb intervals cover both signs of four prime and three square orders. At $p=1000033$, $R=1/4$, the certified defects at normalized mark $0.98$ are bounded by $0.021979n^{1/4}$ on both signs. [Astra's moving-width comparison](astra-transport-22.md) identifies a mean reserve for direct transport and a lower bound on its overshoot cost on the reciprocal zero set. The [separate square certificates](square-pinned-22.md) prove the exact pinned defect through mark one at $q=389^2$, and give exact saturation of the subfield quadratic trial up to an explicit $R$ greater than $0.29226$. The [restricted direction count](parallel-crossratio-22.md) reduces the cell third moment to a specified slope coset and relaxes the cap hypothesis to a finite-index Lorentz bound. The arithmetic check covers five primes. Both prime intermediate-width estimates and the restricted direction bound remain open.

The twenty-third pass establishes [Astra's scaled-tail equivalence](astra-secant-23.md) for ordinary theta and gives a [balanced circulant example](balanced-secant-23.md) where both theta values are correct and the secant limit is two. The [comparison with Potechin–Xu](parallel-graph-shapes-23.md) rules out direct transfer of their noncommutative moment estimates and proves a leading constant of $1/\sqrt2$ for the full fixed-degree path and opposite-point family. The [mixed shapes](mixed-shape-witness-23.md) add 48 fitted rational lower bounds and 18 cross-field evaluations. At $p=4000037$, both fitted bounds exceed $0.7439\sqrt n$. An asymptotic gain for the mixed family remains unproved. The [diagonal pencil decomposition](parallel-directions-23.md) gives a power saving for the parallel pencil and an affine vacancy criterion, verified on six primes and four square controls. The prime scaled-tail bound, the limiting mixed-column support, and the finite-pencil estimate remain open.

The twenty-fourth pass computes the [exact fixed-mark defect curve](pinned-penalty-24.md) from one-sided quadratic values, identifies the finite saturation penalty, and rewrites the field-first target as a weighted squared defect with mean slack. It checks 168 Arb penalty intervals across 42 records. At $p=4000037$ and penalty one, both unrestricted upper bounds are below $0.000161$ and the canonical radius-$n^{1/4}$ lower bounds exceed $0.18$. The arcsine and Mellin calculation proves a necessary canonical correction radius greater than $0.38719\sqrt n$ for a vanishing bounded-radius objective. [Astra's bounded dynamics](astra-dynamics-24.md) gives a field-uniform optimization rate within any specified natural-scale ball and identifies the unsupported limit exchange at the exact mean. The [affine vacancy scan](parallel-vacancy-24.md) verifies the cell-square identity and an exact minimum vacancy fraction of $10/33$ across 4,887 even-subgroup cases in the stated critical window through $10^6$. A prime-uniform estimate for the relaxed defect and an asymptotic vacancy bound remain open.

The twenty-fifth pass proves a [uniform obstruction to a single projected scalar seed](scalar-seed-25.md). In the compact-group limit its negative-part objective is coercive on the full Chebyshev coefficient space and attains a strictly positive minimum when $\mu<a\sqrt2$. This gives a common macroscopic floor as the degree increases after the field limit, and a compact-family obstruction on the actual fields. The sharp scalar test raises the necessary canonical correction radius to more than $0.3872561930\sqrt n$ at mark and mean one. There are 53 floating-point comparison records and two Arb distance bounds. [Astra](astra-seed-25.md) independently excludes small affine-projection cost from positive mixtures of fixed-width reciprocal seeds, with 12 algebra checks. The [cell analysis](parallel-cells-25.md) adds a third/fourth factorial-moment vacancy certificate, a field-first two-tail condition for critical twists, and an exact formulation of the missing extraction step from general bounded-energy witnesses. The [random-circulant audit](parallel-random-circulant-25.md) checks the transferable residual identity and the failure of a direct Gaussian moment replacement. Independent audits checked the scalar and cell arguments. Full projected descent escapes the scalar restriction in the numerical comparisons, but prime-uniform residual decay and the general witness extraction remain open.

The twenty-sixth pass proves a [two-step contraction for actual source-generated increments](parallel-activation-26.md), retaining the root and constant-mode reserve. A sublinear weighted-amplitude envelope uniform in the prime and in depth up to a specified polylogarithmic horizon suffices for the critical residual certificate; a summable increment tail transfers fixed-depth marked cancellation without exchanging limits. Independent audit checked the constants and horizon argument. The 24 floating-point trajectory runs include near-square primes, while ten integer/rational sign audits verify the marked-to-bulk normalization. [Astra's extraction theorem](astra-extraction-26.md) shows that the upper energy assumption is automatic, gives a quantitative mass-preserving truncation under a lower energy bound, and obtains flatness under actual energy saturation. Exact square mixtures and a prime $17$ closure failure delimit two purification operations; their checks replayed independently. The [signed seed program](reciprocal-seed-26.md) compares 216 feasible trials, supplies six saved-family optimum brackets by Arb, and replays their integer payloads without optimization. The [full-Paley construction and anchored finish](parallel-extrapolation-26.md) give a precise limit to a general signed barrier and to small-radius finishing. The prime actual-orbit envelope, the broad witness energy lower bound, and the remaining general-support extraction are open.

The twenty-seventh pass certifies [actual Picard prefixes and harmonic losses](harmonic-orbit-27.md) at primes $9413,153649$ and squares $97^2,389^2$, each through 128 steps. All final shadowing errors are below $1.581\cdot10^{-13}$. Extra root-aware products on the first prime/square pair repeat the same rational paths and retain the mean reserve; at the prime they give a residual upper bound below $4.022\cdot10^{-6}$. A verified invariant ball supplies an all-future norm bound at this one prime. The [time-block lemma](parallel-rate-27.md) combines unequal losses and explains the obstruction from isolated favorable steps. Its independent 192-bit dense audit checks a true 16-step orbit. [Astra's positive-cone gap](astra-increments-27.md) supplies new arithmetic control for nonnegative weights on critical bounded coset unions and applies it to the actual cumulative source. Its checker has 4,800 matrix trials, two interval-certified true first-pair instances, and eight floating-point trajectories. The [signed inverse transport](parallel-transport-27.md) retains trimming costs, critical product requirements, and activation signs; 26 replay audits preserve the exact square-shell controls. Selected certificate, rate, source, and transport outputs replayed byte-for-byte. The broad prime trajectory estimate and the concentration/sign/alternation branches remain open, so Problem 26 is not solved.

The twenty-eighth pass derives a [direct signed-tail certificate](source-tail-28.md) and replays nineteen Arb certificates from saved rational inputs. It preserves the square obstruction and avoids a stationary norm factor. [Astra](astra-broad-source-28.md) supplies a stronger fixed-forcing noncoercivity example, then proves that these same profiles satisfy the new tail criterion. Its five field records contain seven injected profiles and 28 defect comparisons, all independently replayed. The [reciprocal source theorem](parallel-reciprocal-28.md) isolates an exact positive marked test and a separate natural-energy autocorrelation branch; an independent 24-row small-field replay checks its identities. The [integrability audit](parallel-integrability-28.md) supplies a precise functional transfer gap and independently verifies the new cap calculation. The first actual Claude Fable 5.2 High session on this project saves a [positional reformulation and comparison](claude-fable-28.md); its 220 diagnostic records replay exactly. The accepted algebra is field-general. Its proposed prime/square extreme-value transfer remains conditional on an unproved arithmetic premise and does not invalidate the existing sufficient criteria. The actual prime activation-tail estimate remains open, so Problem 26 is not solved.

Round-28 [acceptance metadata](source-tail-28-acceptance.json) distinguishes interval certificates and floating-point identity checks. Main's independent audit corrected the Fable draft's limiting-support equality, equivalence of sup-norm hypotheses and extrapolation beyond tested depths. The formerly pending million-order small-floor proposal completed in round 29 and independently replayed; it is now accepted as the twentieth certificate.

The twenty-ninth pass keeps the root in the [signed-tail error inequality](root-coupled-tail-29.md), obtaining two explicit quadratic constraints and 20 optimizer-free Arb records. It compares the improvement against both the raw old intervals and the old intervals clipped by the already-known root cap. Independent audit verified the algebra, rounding direction and three small replay inputs. All 20 new records replayed byte-for-byte. [Astra's time-window theorem](astra-source-window-29.md) supplies exact signed telescopes, the full energy/mass reserve, a terminal-source sufficient criterion and a linear top-tail obstruction on actual square trajectories. Its 39 rational coefficient/energy cases and 90 floating diagnostics independently replayed. The prime tail estimate remains open; neither finite interval gains nor square lower bounds solve Problem 26.

The round-29 [acceptance record](root-coupled-tail-29-acceptance.json) originally left the cross-side source-channel conversation pending. It completed in round 30. Main independently replayed all 69 records and accepted the finite identities and exact non-Paley obstruction after a separate [proof audit](parallel-audit-30.md). Its first source inverse-moment tightness claim still requires an actual prime leakage estimate.

A separate [short algebra audit](parallel-root-audit-29.md#short-algebra-audit-of-the-paired-reciprocal-inverse) verifies the reciprocal diagonal inverse, normalized constant blocks and the opposite-source Woodbury denominator. It gives an exact opposite Schur-block inverse after subtracting a stationary-source rank-one term. Main checked the constants independently. This preliminary identity alone bounds neither stationary energy and supplies no prime estimate. The completed source-channel result was subsequently accepted in round 30.

The thirtieth pass accepts that completed [coupled-source result](parallel-source-pair-29.md), including its UI-plus-leakage theorem and exact stationary model at energies $E\sim t/2=o(\sqrt n)$. The model has both source UI and a zero marker, but its first inverse source-moment mass concentrates at zero and its marked-row coherence constant diverges. Thus it delimits the reciprocal algebra, without contradicting a prime Paley claim. The [top-tail analysis](top-tail-transfer-30.md) proves the normalized RMS precision loss, a compact sampling lemma, and an arbitrary-error propagation lower bound already on the actual prime kernels. All 16 replay records agree. [Astra's arithmetic audit](astra-arithmetic-coset-30.md) supplies a finite-Artin sufficient theorem and a normalized-integrality reduction, while preserving the general matching premise as unproved. Its 20 records include exact phase/CM controls and five rational cyclic-convolution audits. The [positive-floor depth calculation](parallel-depth-window-30.md) gives an actual simultaneous marked window of iterated-logarithmic size and a square top-tail obstruction on that growing window; its 33 diagnostic/algebra records replay. Main corrected the distinction between absolute and normalized-increment fourth moments in the small-ball transfer condition. A prime tail/leakage estimate, a substantially better error propagation argument, and the nested arithmetic invariant computation remain open. No solution of Problem 26 is asserted.

The round-30 [acceptance record](top-tail-transfer-30-acceptance.json) retains the completed chat identifiers, hashes and arithmetic scope. All three research chats are complete; none is a pending process to restart. The inherited local-inertia/convolution package remains an ordinary mathematical input, not a new Lean verification. Additional million-order nested moments are recorded only as diagnostics, without a limiting-value or prime/square-gap claim.

The thirty-first pass computes genuinely new [nested mixed moments](astra-nested-moment-31.md). Exact character-sum transformations reduce the nested kernel to a reflected rank-three hypergeometric row. Primary-source checks and a separate [audit](parallel-audit-31.md) verify the local blocks, exclusion of rank-one couplings, the two phase-independent invariant counts, and the all-odd-base vertical reality argument. The latter gives a determinant sign and the square cubic main term, while leaving the prime sign open. Main's [full-character lift](nested-lift-31.md) supplies exact aliases and a decomposition into complex cubic correlations. Its nine records independently replay, as do Astra's 23 exact/floating records. The [integer/Arb sign certificates](nested-phase-31.md) rigorously select the positive sign at two finite primes, without selecting it uniformly. The [coherent stationary construction](parallel-coherent-source-31.md) upgrades the previous UI obstruction to a full entry bound and then to actual prime Paley dimensions via a finite-rank orthogonal conjugation. Ten records include two full-matrix checks; the final matrices are explicitly noncirculant and are not small operator-norm perturbations. Neither the arithmetic fixed-word progress nor that abstract counterfamily solves Problem 26. The remaining prime sign/global arithmetic model and an actual Paley tail/leakage estimate remain the next targets.

The round-31 [acceptance record](nested-lift-31-acceptance.json) contains 45 independently replayed records and the completed research-chat identifiers. No process from this round remains pending. The new group arguments and constructions are ordinary mathematical proofs checked against their cited inputs, not a Lean kernel verification; numerical records are not substituted for those arguments.

The thirty-second pass settles the specific arithmetic sign left open in round 31. [Astra's epsilon-factor calculation](astra-prime-phase-32.md) gives $c_q=1$ over every odd finite field, while an [independent mod-four stalk calculation](parallel-audit-32.md) proves the Paley congruence range without high-degree contraction estimates. Main checked the middle-extension endpoint, finite-inertia purity and bad-character Deligne-fiber normalization against the primary sources. The 17 exact determinant replay records check 76 good Mellin characters and 84 ordinary determinant points; the separate 12-record orbit/boundary replay agrees. These finite checks verify normalizations, while the uniform conclusion rests on the written proofs. The cubic moment is now $1/8+O(q^{-1/2})$ through all relevant primes.

The resulting [joint compact model](joint-law-32.md) fixes the entire law of $(x,K(x^2),K(xK(x^2)))$ and all its continuous trace observables. Pointwise compact-group membership, together with equidistribution, also gives matching good-frequency maxima and square-root-size top sums through primes and squares. Twenty exact mixed moments are checked by an independent 81-case Weyl constant-term calculation, with five finite-field diagnostics. This is a proved instance of arithmetic matching for one nested family, not the general matching premise for later activated fields. The [squared-kernel calculation](schur-mixing-32.md) finds the exact $1/4$ cross-projector gap and proves that the coherent rank-twelve counterfamily retains it, excluding a further generic mixing shortcut.

The [actual clipped-source analysis](parallel-circulant-source-32.md) proves the UI control of the unweighted leakage component, identifies the weighted part as an actual signed Paley triangle form, and expresses its soft truncation as a positive integral of bulk hard tails. An explicit response inequality $H(R)^2\le C_\tau\eta(R)$ plus UI suffices for this leakage step. Reusing the previous exact family shows its response ratio diverges linearly, so the new hypothesis excludes that prior obstruction. All 895 source records independently replay, with only the 19 runtime values omitted from exact JSON comparisons. Neither this response inequality nor UI is proved through primes, and a subsequent marked comparison still remains. Problem 26 is not solved.

The round-32 [acceptance record](nested-joint-32-acceptance.json) separates 56 exact records, 895 floating/algebraic-limit records and 19 runtime records, 970 in total, together with proof-audit scope and completed research-chat identifiers. The uniform phase and joint-law conclusions are ordinary mathematical arguments with the cited sheaf inputs; they are not Lean kernel proofs. The next mathematical targets are an actual prime hard-tail/UI estimate or a structured growing-depth argument, and arithmetic closure beyond this first rank-three family.

The thirty-third pass keeps the UI and hard-tail tasks separate. [Astra's actual hot theorem](astra-hardtail-33.md) proves all-threshold HR with the sharp order $\Theta(\sqrt\tau)$ of its floor dependence, using the existing hot invariant ball. It also proves an actual weighted-operator obstruction and constructs an exactly cyclic reciprocal UI family whose HR ratio grows linearly. That family lacks Paley coherence and sector dimensions; it is not a prime Paley counterexample. Rigorous enclosures certify 2280 threshold knots on 18 cold stationary sides, plus a hot finite witness showing that the constant cannot always be one. Main independently replayed all 84 primary records.

The [UI boundary-slope theorem](parallel-prime-ui-33.md) turns the missing first-mass estimate into a precise convex program with primal and weak-dual certificates. Main checked the disjoint high-set argument, the $\mathcal D/\kappa$ coupling, the field-first boundary limit, and the retained mark contribution. Exact saved Fourier inputs rigorously bound the actual million-order prime source by $6.90943062$ and $7.57382883$ at floors $.05$ and $.01$; the square control has strictly positive certified tail mass through threshold 128. Twenty-four exact-source interval rows and thirteen conic bracket rows independently replay. The separate 1350 trajectory diagnostic rows remain labelled floating comparisons; they are not used as uniform or stationary-distance certificates.

The [positive Schur argument](parallel-audit-33.md) adds an automatic inequality on the actual clipped source and distinguishes near-Markov covariance kernels from their precision matrices. Forty normalization records independently replay. Main's [complex-floor analysis](complex-floor-33.md) gives a separate conditional finish through harmonic measure, a quantitative square-field obstruction to a field-general analytic shortcut, and a fully checked right-half-plane branch at prime 13. Its seven exact/Arb records replay without an optimizer. None of these results proves the cold prime-uniform HR estimate, the conic $o(\kappa)$ rate, or the subsequent marked arithmetic comparison. The full ETH26 objective remains open.

The round-33 [acceptance record](source-ui-33-acceptance.json) records the independently verified core subset, exact saved witness hashes, proof-audit scope, and completed chat handles. It distinguishes finite interval certificates from asymptotic proofs and from unreplayed exploratory diagnostics. A stronger rate such as $d_p(\kappa)=O_\tau(\kappa^2)$, a cold actual-source response estimate, or prime-specific complex continuation would each provide a concrete next advance; none is silently assumed.

The thirty-fourth pass obtains an actual positive construction lemma rather than another reformulation of UI. [Astra's clipped reflection](astra-conic-primal-34.md) preserves both large marks while repairing the bulk floor, and quantifies the product-Hellinger displacement from the clipped pair. Main and the independent auditor checked the reflection, diagonal/coherence constants, normalized mass factors, and the bulk-only cap. The rate requires the explicit deficit-height and cap hypotheses; it is not a bound on the total conic gap without a separate clipped-gap estimate. Five positive exact-input certificates and one square rejection replay at 224 bits. The proposal trajectories are floating provenance; the rigorous constructions start at the saved rational Fourier rows.

The [joint Schur route](parallel-schur-response-34.md) strengthens the CP bound to a whole threshold Gram matrix and combines it with exact Paley difference counts. Its corrected trace has a positive linear limit at the actual zero hinge, even though the true response there stays bounded. This rules out that trace replacement as a uniform HR proof, while leaving source-vector signed bounds open. A new continuous interval cover rigorously certifies every real threshold at four distinct cold primes through $153649$; the stronger $40009$ run has ratio below $.456$. Main independently replayed all 154 records, excluding only elapsed-time fields from deterministic equality.

Main's [two-variable complex curves](complex-branches-34.md) extend the prime-$13$ example to $17$ and square order $25$. An [independent exact audit](parallel-audit-34.md) checks the actual sector templates, both stationary equations, discarded factors, and the removable equal-weight point, and proves that every coordinate of the full reciprocal pair is nonzero and holomorphic on the right half-plane. All 31 main/independent records replay. The square example is a useful control: finite continuation alone supplies no prime/square distinction or growth estimate.

The three paths now have different next obligations. The conic path needs prime-uniform proposal fidelity and signed spill; the Schur path needs a source-vector estimate that retains cancellation beyond the trace; the complex path needs common domains and quantitative growth over growing primes. None supplies the others automatically, and HR/UI still leave the regularized marked comparison to prove. The [round-34 acceptance record](repair-response-34-acceptance.json) retains 193 independently replayed core records, six exact witness hashes, proof scope and completed chat identifiers. The 74 exploratory conic trajectory rows are recorded separately and are not promoted to uniform evidence. ETH26 remains open.

The thirty-fifth pass changes the constructive target. Main's [averaged-mark budget](averaged-mark-35.md) combines the genuine Picard energy telescope, the older projected-average witnesses, and the round-34 mean/mark-preserving repair. The exact budget tends to $1+\tau+1/T$. If positive completion works through large primes, the inherited fixed-depth marked cancellation gives a direct theta squeeze; no additional conic fidelity objective, UI, HR or inverse comparison is required on this route. An independent audit verified the sharper constant, the fixed/growing limit orders and the Cesaro marked hypothesis. Five derived finite theta bounds reuse accepted round-34 positive inputs; they are not new large positivity computations.

The same [independent audit](parallel-audit-35.md) proves that the saved square minus mark exceeds its exact feasible maximum by a positive rational amount. This excludes every nonnegative mean-one minus completion preserving that mark, strengthening the earlier single-reflection rejection. At large square orders, the averaged proposal has the same obstruction whenever $\tau+1/T<1$. On the original inverse-source route, the audit obtains sector-preserving $L^1$ approximants and an exact forcing-cancellation limitation of positive-floor regularization; it does not remove the stationary arithmetic dependency.

[Astra's signed-scatter theorem](astra-signed-repair-35.md) is an actual arithmetic bound for a specified quadratic profile. A separate determinant-constrained corner argument extends the $1/\sqrt2$ normalized-mark barrier to the first nonscalar nested affine family and handles arbitrary field-dependent coefficients. Main checked the pinning errors, corner realization, coefficient-uniform transfer and positive-density lower bound. Those results neither identify the true clipped deficit nor exclude nonlinear deep repairs. The 74 diagnostic rows, including the newly suggested averaged proposals, replay but remain floating; positive entries observed there are not interval-certified.

The [complex-structure work](parallel-complex-structure-35.md) obtains a genuine common hot complex domain using the already accepted mixed Mellin estimate. ## Round 180

The paired invariant \(J\) is the cross-ratio \([\pi';\pi,\rho,\rho']\) of the two lines. The classes \(J=0\) and \(J=1\) are exact point coincidences, and together they cost \(O(p^{2399/1280})\). Each generic class costs \(O(p^{15/8})\), but these bounds are positive and cannot be summed, so the whole diagonal stays at \(O(p^{9/4})\). [J cross-ratio](ordinary-paired-J-cross-ratio-180.md)

For every \(\lambda\ne1\), the row autocorrelation is \(O(p^{11/8})\), by a genuine line family and the Stevens–de Zeeuw incidence bound. This gives \(\sum_\xi W_\xi^{3/2}=O(p^{43/16})\), right energy \(O(p^{43/16})\) and one-match covariance \(O(p^{115/64})\). The energy route cannot pass \(p^{13/8}\) when \(M_1\asymp p\). [Row autocorrelation](ordinary-mixed-row-autocorrelation-180.md)

The normalized \(R_{602}\) lies between zero and \(9.07\cdot10^{-209}\), so the growing cold prefix holds exactly when its increments stay delocalized. An \(n\)-independent sup/RMS bound (Criterion D) is proved sufficient, but not necessary. [Cold prefix](cold-growing-prefix-delocalization-180.md)

The GPT gateway account was out of quota. Native Claude subagents ran the three missions, and a separate agent audited them. The user asked to stop after this round. [Review](native-claude-review-180.md) · [Acceptance](round-180-acceptance.json) · [Coordinator](../coordination/round-180.json)

## Tools and working method

- At the user's request, each research window closes with one or two independently ranked next steps from each route. Ask for the decisive mathematical quantity and what would confirm or rule out the proposed step. Main checks the premises, consolidates overlaps, and records the resulting priorities in the acceptance and ledger before dispatching another full window.
- The [Conjecture Search template by Brosse, Li and Lyu](https://human-ai-mathematics.github.io/conjecture-search/proposal.html) supplies the useful pattern of a fixed target, one ledger, saved computations and separately reviewable proof notes. This project uses that structure locally. The upstream [template revision inspected](https://github.com/human-ai-mathematics/conjecture-search-template/tree/3d6708a86c1aa895d890b09cf26e84579c807765) also offers MyST and agent roles.
- [VibeMath](https://github.com/cyanseek/VibeMath) offers a read-only frontier index and replay handoffs. [Jig #7](https://jig.so/p/7) has the relevant partial claims for this specific conjecture. [OpenTorus's Paley campaign](https://github.com/maximilianbehr/OpenTorus/tree/main/examples/paley-clique) targets the polylogarithmic clique conjecture and describes a per-prime localization LP. Its campaign driver uses Docker and a separate model backend. [do-math](https://github.com/MegasKomnenos/do-math) is geared toward unattended multi-session campaigns. The local scripts here replay the computations directly.
- The main Codex conversation maintains this ledger and checks the separate research conversations. The first round covers [sparse sampling](parallel-sparse.md), [stationary spectra](parallel-stationary.md) and [independent audit](parallel-audit.md). The second covers [Jacobi arithmetic](parallel-arithmetic-2.md), [stationary coupling](parallel-coupling-2.md) and [random sparse marks](parallel-random-2.md). The third covers [exterior compression](parallel-exterior-3.md), [anchored overlaps](parallel-overlap-3.md) and [sparse dual majorants](parallel-cost-3.md). The fourth covers [signed time evolution](parallel-sign-4.md), [fixed-depth reference forms](parallel-reference-4.md) and [paired anchors](parallel-five-4.md). The fifth covers [signed windows](parallel-half-modes-5.md), [reciprocal spectra](parallel-half-arithmetic-5.md) and [Krylov certificates](parallel-half-krylov-5.md). The sixth covers [capped finite-time transfer](parallel-finite-time-6.md), [reflected energy](parallel-extreme-6.md) and [small Fourier witness families](parallel-witness-6.md). A separate ChatGPT session explored the public problem. [Comparison with the local routes](chatgpt-2026-11-17.md).
- [Faure's 2024 ETH thesis](https://doi.org/10.3929/ethz-b-000694627), Sections 2.3 and 3.2, records the older random-circulant concentration and discrepancy routes. Repairing Fourier positivity after changing support increases the diagonal by the deleted absolute row mass. This does not directly transfer to a sharp prime Paley certificate. The present smoothing route uses complementary supports to avoid relying on the deleted mass.
- A 2026-11-18 source refresh still finds Conjecture 26 in the [ETH statement](https://randomstrasse101.math.ethz.ch/posts/PaleyGraph/). [Chen et al. v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22971v1) concerns independent-bit random circulant graphs, and [Potechin–Xu v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30064v1) concerns dense random graphs. Their stated conclusions are not prime Paley estimates; no new solution of Problem 26 was established by this source check.
